## Chapter 16: Labs for Cockroaches

**chapter_id**: `chapter-16-labs-for-cockroaches`
**word_count**: 2500
**sections**: 5

- 16.1 Advanced Panels
- 16.2 What These Tests Cannot Do
- 16.3 What These Tests Can Do
- 16.4 The Catch
- 16.5 What Did We Learn Here?

### Body

Your blood work came back normal? Congratulations. So did the Titanic's hull inspection.

I treated patients through COVID and never caught the virus. Nasal swabbed and PCR'd every week. Negative every time. I was a Level III Cockroach. Hard to kill.

Then came the booster.

Did I want it? No. Was I afraid of the virus or the vaccine? Also no. I was annoyed.

A surfer in Malibu was arrested for paddling into the ocean alone. Handcuffs in the name of public health. Fear had become policy. Policy had become performance. Performance had become profit.

Human beings are arrogant little creatures. We think we can control every variable that makes life uncomfortable. Predators. Tornadoes. Broken air conditioning. Bad coffee.

Life is a gift. The cost of that gift is that someday you are going to die. Best come to terms with that now and find joy every day you are upright.

The pandemic had no use for joy.

Health care workers were placed in an impossible position. For the first time in modern medicine, we were told to send patients home and wait until they crashed. Do nothing. No early IV support. No aggressive risk stratification. No conversation about vitamin D, metabolic health, thrombosis risk, or baseline inflammation. Providing an early and inexpensive dexamethasone shot could get you in trouble. The data later indicated it was a smart move in high-risk patients. Forget adjunct measures or off-label use. Ivermectin won the 2015 Nobel Prize in Medicine. Then it became horse paste. Suggesting the spike protein might contribute to coagulation problems was misinformation. Questioning the origin of the virus could end your career.

Then came the vaccines. Emergency Use Authorization. Not full approval. A crisis pathway built for moments when the government says no adequate approved alternative exists.

The country split itself into two new religions overnight.

My anti-vax patients warned me about Event 201, injectable nanobots, and Bill Gates' secret plan to thin the herd. My pro-vax patients preached shutdowns and moral purity while accepting liquor stores as essential services. Former hippies became unpaid pharmaceutical sales reps who could not name a single ingredient in the product they were defending. Right-wing conservatives voiced a sudden distrust of corporations, federal agencies, and medical consensus.

Months earlier, both tribes had been meeting for drinks and doing cocaine off the same toilet seats. Then they swapped jerseys and became experts in public health.

My take was an unpopular opinion among both sides, so I kept my mouth shut. To me, COVID was an obvious cash grab and political power play. It was medically lazy. But COVID was also real.

It was highly transmissible. It killed the elderly, the obese, diabetics, and patients with cardiovascular disease at rates well above background. The same comorbidities drive respiratory mortality every year. Influenza does it. RSV does it. A common cold turns into secondary pneumonia and kills elderly grandmas in droves every year.

COVID was not rabies. Rabies is 100% fatal and the vaccine is 100% effective. If a rabid animal bites you, take the series. No podcast. No prayer circle. No internet research. Take the shots.

COVID was nuanced. The risk was real but unevenly distributed. The science was evolving but the messaging pretended it was settled. The public health response became a loyalty test. The pharmaceutical opportunity became historic.

Big Pharma was about to clear a hundred billion dollars. Moderna stock was going to 10x. Everyone paying attention knew it.

I did not buy any. It did not feel right. Neither did the mandates.

Take the injection or lose your hospital privileges. A command, not a conversation. Not informed consent. Not individualized risk. Not medicine as an art practiced between doctor and patient.

I was tired of being the squeaky wheel. I complied.

And for my trouble, I got myocarditis. Inflammation of the heart. I told myself I was fine. A little scar tissue. No big deal. Plenty of myocardium to go around.

Pride goeth before the fall.

The brain fog moved in quietly. Not the dramatic kind. The read-the-same-paragraph-four-times kind. The stare-at-the-question-and-know-you-know-the-answer-but-cannot-retrieve-it kind.

My wit was cloudy, but Neurology said I was clear. Standard labs excellent. Specialists suggested anxiety. Stress. Burnout. A normal part of aging. One colleague told me I was having a midlife crisis, go buy a convertible.

But I knew my own brain. I could still deduce the correct answer on exams. It just took longer to find it. I was still working, still passing, still performing.

That is the problem with early dysfunction. You can be sick long before you are obviously disabled. Standard medicine is very good at detecting catastrophe. It is often terrible at detecting trajectory.

Now I was in the same predicament as many of my patients. I needed advanced diagnostics. A can of worms I did not want to open. Reluctantly, I paid cash for a Vibrant Wellness Neural Zoomer and a Tickborne 2.0 Panel. About a grand in total.

The results came back loaded.

Red flags I had never seen in anyone. Anti-dopamine receptor 1 at greater than 30. Anti-dopamine receptor 2 at 20.6. Anti-glycine receptor at 21.1. Anti-Streptococcal A at 20.2. PANDAS pattern in a grown man. Viral reactivation across the board.

Most concerning was anti-CASPR2 at greater than 30. Associated with Morvan syndrome, neuromyotonia, and limbic encephalitis. Rare. Bad. The kind of report you reread, then go very quiet.

All of it sitting in my blood while every standard workup told me I was fine.

So I did what cockroaches do. I adapted. More sun. Longer fasts. Heavier weights. Cleaner diet. Hot sauna. NAD+. Omega-3s. Astaxanthin. Magnesium.

The cockroach adapts. But the cockroach also knows when a storm is coming.

My efforts were not enough. The storm hit.

Weakness. Dysphagia. Double vision. Within days I could barely stand.

My immune system was shredding my nerves. Parts of me were paralyzed. The diagnosis was not simple, nor was it good. We will get into it in Chapter 22. This chapter is about advanced testing, because sometimes the standard map is not enough. Sometimes the terrain is burning before the satellite image updates.

I had the labs before the paralysis. I knew what they meant. I underestimated COVID.

In the general public, the numbers eventually surfaced. Myocarditis after the second mRNA dose ran roughly 1 in 5,000 in young males, higher in adolescents. The lab-leak hypothesis became the official assessment of the FBI and Department of Energy by 2023. Excess deaths from delayed care during the lockdowns ran into the hundreds of thousands. Other damage will never fit cleanly into a spreadsheet: suicides, developmental losses, missed diagnoses, addiction relapses, broken trust, and children who spent their formative years behind masks.

Each began as a forbidden question. Then the evidence accumulated. Then the institutions quietly adjusted their language.

That is how gaslighting ends. Not with an apology. With a footnote.

Advanced labs do not make you invincible. They do not replace clinical judgment. But in the right context, they show you the war before the body collapses.

They are headlights.

Sometimes headlights are the difference between making the turn and driving into the ravine.

16.1 Advanced Panels

You do not need every advanced test on the menu. You need to know which ones reveal what the standard panel cannot. These days, you have AI to help you discern your condition. But it takes a good prompt to get a good answer. These are some useful prompts.

GI-MAP (Microbial Assay Plus). The most neglected test in medicine. PCR-based stool analysis that maps pathogenic bacteria, opportunistic overgrowth, parasites, fungi, viral markers, digestive enzyme function, and gut barrier integrity. Brain fog, mood swings, autoimmune flares, unexplained inflammation. The answer often starts in the gut. The GI-MAP finds it.

DUTCH (Dried Urine Test for Comprehensive Hormones). Our naturopathic doctor's favorite test, especially for women. A blood test tells you how much estrogen is in the tank. The DUTCH tells you whether it's being routed through the protective 2-OH pathway or the DNA-damaging 4-OH pathway. It maps your full daily cortisol curve instead of the morning snapshot. It catches HPA axis dysfunction that gets dismissed as "you're just stressed."

Mycotoxin and Heavy Metal Panels. A water-damaged apartment twenty years ago. Amalgam fillings releasing mercury every time you chew. Lead from old paint or the shooting range. These rarely show on a standard panel. They show on urinary mycotoxin panels through Great Plains, Real Time, or Vibrant. On blood through MyMycoLabs. A colleague once told me mold toxicity was not real. He missed the Forensic Files where three infants died from Stachybotrys. Black mold is real. And dangerous. Environmental toxins are part of the reason autoimmune disease has tripled in thirty years.

Tickborne Disease Panel. Vibrant's covers Lyme, Babesia, Bartonella, Anaplasma, Ehrlichia, even toxoplasmosis which is more common than assumed. IGeneX is the gold standard for Lyme as it comes with FDA clearance. Standard Quest and Labcorp two-tier testing miss a meaningful percentage. Lyme is one of the most contested diagnoses in medicine. Some people clear the infection and never know they had it. Others get prompt antibiotics and feel fine afterwards. Then there is the 14 percent who suffer from persistent fatigue, body pain, and cognitive impairment. According to Johns Hopkins.

Coronary Artery Calcium (CAC) Scan. One hundred dollars. Five minutes. A score of zero means your 10-year cardiac risk is roughly 1 percent. A score above 300 puts you in the same risk tier as someone who already had a heart attack. The 2026 ACC/AHA guidelines formally use CAC to set LDL targets. Your cholesterol panel does not tell you if you have heart disease. There are many ways to improve heart health without defaulting to a statin. Especially if your CAC score is low.

Full-Body MRI. A rich person's favorite. Prenuvo, Ezra, Craft Health. One scan, no radiation, screens for solid tumors, aneurysms, and organ abnormalities. Around $2,500. Survivors call it lifesaving. Critics call it overdiagnosis. Both are right.

Prenuvo's own data shows about half of scans turn up something that needs follow-up. Roughly 13 percent recommend urgent workup. About 2 percent find something potentially life-threatening. Find the cancer at stage 1 instead of stage 4 and the math is unbeatable. Find a benign cyst that triggers a biopsy that triggers a hospital stay and you have invented a problem you did not have. Order the scan with a plan, or don't order it at all.

Biological Age and Genetic Testing. Epigenetic clocks (TruDiagnostic, GlycanAge) and whole genome sequencing (under $300). Interesting data, easy to oversell. Bio age moves with a bad week of sleep. Genetic testing is the more useful one-and-done.

One genome sequence tells you whether you are an APOE4 carrier with eight to twelve times the Alzheimer's risk. It tells you whether you are a CYP2D6 poor metabolizer who should never be prescribed codeine. Whether a statin will cause you muscle pain. Whether aspirin is unnecessary risk. One test. Decades of decisions.

Biological age moves with a hangover. Your genome does not.

The appendix lists more. Organic Acids. CIRS panels. Methylation. Pharmacogenomics. Galleri multi-cancer screening. Use them when the case demands it. Comprehensive labs are usually sufficient. Keep these in your back pocket. They could help you or a loved one get to a real diagnosis without the usual delay.

16.2 What These Tests Cannot Do

Sensitivity versus specificity. A test that catches everyone catches a lot of false positives. Vibrant's microarray is screening-grade. Like a metal detector. Excellent at flagging something buried. Imperfect at telling you exactly what is there. Confirm critical findings with a gold-standard cell-based assay before building a treatment plan.

Antibodies are footprints, not fires. An IgG antibody shows you were exposed. It does not always mean you are currently sick. Antibody titers from one moldy apartment can linger long after you moved out. Same with food allergies.

Most are not FDA-cleared. They are CLIA-certified, meaning the lab is regulated for analytical accuracy. Most specialty panels are not FDA-approved for diagnostic use. This is a paperwork distinction that lets insurance deny coverage. It does not mean the science is bad.

The provider matters more than the test. A Vibrant Neural Zoomer in the hands of a clinician who has never seen one is an expensive paperweight. In the hands of one who has run hundreds, it is the test that catches your autoimmune storm years before the ICU.

When standard labs say you are fine and your body says otherwise, you have the next move.

16.3 What These Tests Can Do

Find the missing piece. Irritable bowel syndrome resolves when GI-MAP reveals a Bifidobacterium longum deficiency and the right strain goes back in. Chronic diarrhea clears after a binder pulls out the bile acids the gallbladder can no longer manage. Ten years of "fibromyalgia" turns out to be Bartonella that responds to four months of doxycycline and rifampin.

Mold toxicity. Smoldering infection. SIBO. CIRS. Mast cell disorders. Heavy metal toxicity from a hobby nobody asked about. These are real conditions that are often dismissed because the path to restoration is time-consuming and difficult.

The most challenging patients are these subacute ones. They feel significantly unwell, yet they are not sick enough for conventional medicine to care. That patient rides the medical merry-go-round. The right panel ends the dismissal in a single blood draw.

16.4 The Catch

Have a plan. No need to test for testing's sake. If an overly sensitive test flags an elevated histamine, now you are being treated for a syndrome you do not have. Find the provider who orders advanced labs selectively, interprets them honestly, and tells you when the result is a false positive instead of selling you seventeen supplements.

That provider is hard to find.

It is about to get a lot easier.

16.5 What Did We Learn Here?

This chapter was not about COVID. It was about the health sovereignty movement that exploded in the aftermath. Sovereignty was a small sector before 2020. In 2026 it is the future of medicine. The largest exodus from reactive medicine to proactive. To some degree, it happened because the institutions earned it. The system did not collapse. It just lost the monopoly.

Health sovereignty is not a marketing term. It is a return to first principles. The cockroach has been running it for 300 million years.

In 2018, researchers at South China Normal University sequenced the full genome of the American cockroach and published their findings in Nature Communications. They discovered that its genome is larger than the human genome, with massively expanded gene families for immunity, detoxification, and tissue regeneration. When infected, cockroach cells secrete their own antimicrobial peptides directly into the hemolymph. They manufacture their own antibiotics. They regrow lost limbs in a single molting cycle. In China, they call the cockroach Xiao Qiang. It translates to "little mighty one."

This is where medicine is headed. Test your mettle. See what you are made of.

You are the little mighty one.

— End of Chapter 16 —

## Chapter 17: The Roach Coach

**chapter_id**: `chapter-17-the-roach-coach`
**word_count**: 1616
**sections**: 4

- 17.1 The Grift
- 17.2 The Version That Did Not Make the Front Page
- 17.3 Wizard of Awe
- 17.4 Your Move

### Body

This chapter was written by AI. Sort of.

AI isn't coming. It's already here. The Cockroach Diet uploaded three decades of advanced protocols, personalized medicine, and clinical data on regenerative medicine and built the Roach Coach. It only took a weekend.

The Roach Coach is AI Predictive Analysis based on your biomarkers, physiology, and genetics. Not a chatbot with a wellness sticker slapped on its forehead. Not a fake doctor in avatar form. Not a digital funnel designed to sell you a subscription, a peptide, or a cancellation process devised by Satan's paralegal.

An actual tool. More comprehensive than any book. More patient than most humans. Available 24/7. And it works on your case for free.

Our AI-powered medical sherpa is trained on our protocols, our research, and, hopefully, our ethics. Upload your labs and get a plain-English breakdown. Ask about a symptom and get a flowchart. Feed it your sleep scores, workout log, food journal, or the research paper you did not have time to read. It builds a smart chart that belongs to you, not an insurance company. Bonus: it will scold you for eating seed oils.

We called it SHERPA because that's the job. It carries the load. It knows the terrain. It gets you to the next checkpoint. It does not summit for you.

Billionaires are already fine-tuning their own AI health platforms. We built ours for fun. And to prove a point. The technology to take control of your health journey is here. It is accessible. It is not expensive. You do not need to be a rich person chasing immortality to optimize your wellness.

The future of health is inspiring. It is also concerning. The same intelligence that created SHERPA can also create a $1.8 billion greed machine that profits off bad medicine. Same tools. Same acceleration. Completely different outcome.

17.1 The Grift

On April 2, 2026, The New York Times published a business profile America loves. The story featured a 41-year-old Los Angeles entrepreneur, his brother, a handful of AI tools, and a company suddenly worth more than a hospital. The headline read, "How A.I. Helped One Man (and His Brother) Build a $1.8 Billion Company."

The company was Medvi. The product was compounded GLP-1 weight-loss medication. Sold directly to consumers at the exact moment half the country was in an Ozempic craze. For the record, "Lose weight fast, cheap and easy" is always a scam. Now you know.

ChatGPT helped write the code. Midjourney generated the ads. ElevenLabs voiced the sales calls. The numbers were astonishing. $401 million in 2025 revenue, 250,000 customers, and a projected $1.8 billion in 2026.

It was the perfect story for the age. Artificial intelligence, obesity, telehealth, hustle culture, and the promise that software could finally do what the American healthcare system had failed to do.

Move quickly.

There was only one problem.

The story was a dumpster fire before the profile ever ran.

Six weeks earlier, the FDA issued Warning Letter #721455 to Medvi, citing the company for misbranding its GLP-1 products and implying agency approvals it did not have. Nearly a year before the Times profile, Futurism exposed more than 800 fake doctor accounts used in Medvi's advertising ecosystem. The ads featured AI-generated faces and fabricated medical credentials. When reporters contacted a physician listed on Medvi's website, he said he was not involved and asked to be removed.

The goal was to make money. To do that, Medvi simulated synthetic authority in a white coat, on blast.

There were fake transformations. Before-and-after photos with face-swap tools recycled as Medvi patients. "Michael P." claimed he lost 48 pounds in five months on Medvi's medication. The images came from a Reddit user whose original post detailed his sobriety journey. AI just stole his image and his story.

Then came the infrastructure problems. OpenLoop Health, Medvi's clinical partner, suffered a 1.6-million-record data breach exposing patient names, addresses, and medical histories. A Delaware lawsuit accused OpenLoop and its compounding partner Triad Rx of selling oral tirzepatide that was not absorbed at therapeutic levels. A separate California class-action, James v. Medvi LLC, accused the company of flooding inboxes with spam through spoofed domains and falsified email headers. Trustpilot, Reddit, and the Better Business Bureau detailed complaints of surprise $299 charges, failed cancellations, and nightmare refund processes.

Gallagher admitted to the Times that the company's AI chatbots suffered from hallucinations. The machine was inventing products. People were buying them.

Backlash to the puff piece was swift. Seven days after the profile, Gallagher blamed affiliates for the fake-doctor ads. His company kept selling. Kept growing. Kept pushing. The roadmap was bigger than weight loss. Men's health. Women's hormones. Hair restoration. Peptides. Longevity. Hope.

The algorithm could maximize every insecurity. Every soft belly. Every bald spot. Every aging face. Every desperate late-night search from a person who feels broken and wants to feel healthy again. That was the market.

Artificial intelligence helped Gallagher turn regulatory gray zones, human desperation, synthetic trust, and medical ambiguity into a billion-dollar extraction engine.

The grift is not that AI entered medicine.

The grift is that the first people to scale it were not interested in healing. They were interested in conversion.

17.2 The Version That Did Not Make the Front Page

In the same month, a very different kind of AI medicine quietly came online. This revolution would not be televised.

Eli Lilly flipped the switch on LillyPod, the most powerful supercomputer ever owned by a pharmaceutical company. Over a thousand NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra GPUs, fed with hundreds of terabytes of proprietary genomic data, built to do one thing. Invent new drugs at a scale and speed once unimaginable.

The old model of drug discovery was a brutal decade-long slog. Expensive. Failure-prone. Economically hostile to rare diseases.

AI rewrites the equation. Iambic Therapeutics is moving AI-designed molecules into human trials with striking speed. Neural networks dream up the candidate. Robots synthesize it. Humans test it. The new pipeline feels less like research and more like a factory with a nervous system. It can scan biochemical haystacks no human team could ever touch and return precise solutions.

For patients with obscure diseases that once attracted zero investment, this is hope. For those scientists who once did the imagining, it may be time to consider retirement.

Chemists go first. Diagnosticians are next.

Google's Med-Gemini processes X-rays, pathology slides, genomic sequences, lab results, and even messy handwritten clinical notes in a single multimodal pass. It connects dots across specialties the way an exceptional clinician does on their best day. Except this machine does not sleep.

Expertise becomes harder to hoard. A child in rural Montana can access a second opinion that surpasses the best Manhattan concierge practice. Radiologists, pathologists, and general practitioners gain a co-pilot they never requested but cannot outrun.

Then comes your bathroom.

Smart toilets are turning waste into data. Computer vision and AI analyze stool for hydration, consistency, and early biomarkers of gastrointestinal, kidney, or metabolic trouble. Stanford-inspired models track trends over time, spotting patterns long before your gastroenterologist has an opening.

Detect earlier. Adapt faster. Extend healthy lifespan.

AI is leverage at scale. It does not have a conscience. The only question is who wields it. The ethical healer or the slick marketer. Clean science or a sophisticated trap.

Every patient alive today is stepping into a new kind of wilderness. The old predators had teeth. The new ones have chatbots.

17.3 Wizard of Awe

The machines amplify whoever is behind them.

Our Roach Coach will not sell you peptides. It will not trap you in a subscription. It will not hit your credit card with a phantom $299 charge after you "cancel." There is nothing to purchase inside it. No affiliates. No fake doctors. No face swaps. No funnel pretending to be medicine.

Just clinical pattern recognition, organized. Programmed to be helpful. Not medical advice. That’s still illegal without a consult.

The prototype came together fast. The judgment behind it did not. That took decades of regenerative protocols, emergency medicine algorithms, strange cases, failed assumptions, hard lessons, and endless refinements.

It is not your doctor. But even better than a diagnosis or a prescription, it will help you understand more about your health than perhaps anyone on the planet.

In the coming age, that is the most important product.

17.4 Your Move

In the same way that kerosene lamps gave way to electric lightbulbs, the dinosaurs of yesterday will die. The cockroaches will thrive.

Three shifts are converging at the same time. Diagnosis is going instant. Drug discovery is going synthetic. Monitoring is going ambient.

Your ring, your watch, your mattress, your scale, your toilet, your steering wheel will stream passive data while you live your life. That data can be tracked, trended, and personalized on an AI system.

The same tools that built the grift can also build the antidote. Custom protocols and interventions based on genetics and biomarkers will be delivered to your door. Just like junk food, wellness will become cheap and convenient. All you have to do is choose which outcome you desire most.

AI wrote this chapter for me. It did a decent job. It forgot to mention digital twins, the personalized nanotechnology already in trials, and the molecule designed for your specific disease before you know you have it. You can ChatGPT all that.

The point still lands. By 2030, the average patient will have access to more personalized pattern recognition than the Mayo Clinic. The Roach Coach is one early version. There will be hundreds. Most will be free.

Medicine may finally become what it was always supposed to be.

Personal. Precise. Humane.

For everyone else, there's Medvi.

— End of Chapter 17 —

## Chapter 18: The 12-Week Rollout

**chapter_id**: `chapter-18-the-12-week-rollout`
**word_count**: 1646
**sections**: 6

- 18.1 Twelve Weeks Is a Lie
- 18.2 Mike and Diane
- 18.3 The Three P's
- 18.4 The Toolbox
- 18.5 The 12-Week Example
- 18.6 What Twelve Weeks Buys You

### Body

The lab-verified, condition-specific, week-by-week rollout treats you like a clinical trial of one.

Wrestling icon Hulk Hogan dominated the '80s. His tagline was almost perfect. Train hard. Take your vitamins. Say your prayers. Believe in yourself.

The piece he was missing was a guy in his corner telling him to retire the Atomic Leg Drop. His finishing move was a flying body slam onto his own sacrum. Decades of impact destroyed his spine. A sleeper hold would have been just as dramatic. And it may have reduced the surgeries and pain pills in his later years.

The Hulkster did not have The Cockroach Diet. You do. The homework is complete. You have all the knowledge to begin the 12-Week Rollout. At the end, you may find all you needed was one simple tweak. A single lifestyle modification that changes your health trajectory. Catastrophe averted. More often, a tag-team is needed. STARTED. Supplements. Mitochondria. Hormones. Gut. Hormesis. Longevity. One after another, in the right order, for the win.

The formula is undefeated.

18.1 Twelve Weeks Is a Lie

The 12-Week Rollout is a marketing hook. You’re never one and done.

The protocol recommendations in this chapter are synthesized from clinical guidelines, exercise physiology literature, sleep research, nutritional biochemistry, and regenerative medicine references listed in the chapter endnotes.

Twelve weeks is the length of one cycle. The cycle is repeated indefinitely, with metrics that change and a framework that doesn't.

The framework functions as a metaheuristic. That means TCD is a high-level operating system that finds optimal solutions inside the body it is currently running on. It measures, adapts and intervenes as needed. It is always needed.

STARTED is a useful protocol. Daily, repeatable, foundational. But protocols are static. Bodies are dynamic. Maximum health and longevity require constant attention. Targeted repair and software updates are necessary. Just like your phone. Otherwise, the system gets buggy.

Imagine two patients walk into the clinic on the same morning. They both begin the 12-week rollout. They leave with very different instructions. They both have beneficial outcomes. One takes longer and requires more work than the other. Master the process and the mission takes care of itself.

18.2 Mike and Diane

Mike is 47. Tech executive. Two kids. Twenty-five pounds over his wedding weight. Tired by 2 PM. Libido gone. Vague depression even though his work-life balance is solid. He loves his family. He enjoys ocean fishing and pickleball. But he's not firing on all cylinders. At his last physical, his primary care doctor told him he was "fine" and suggested he sleep more and stress less.

Comprehensive analysis said otherwise. Fasting insulin 18. Testosterone 280, free testosterone in the gutter. SHBG and hs-CRP elevated. Vitamin D 21. The typical American executive at midlife. Not sick enough for the system to care. Heading there fast.

Mike is not hard to fix. Millions of him are walking into regenerative clinics looking for hormone optimization, mitochondrial support, and a provider who cares enough to guide the process.

Diane is harder.

She's 58. Retired professional. Sharp, educated, more informed about her own condition than most doctors. She has long COVID, POTS, chronic vertigo no specialist can explain, and a gut destroyed by C. difficile colitis. The C. diff was a parting gift from the antibiotics that were supposed to fix chronic Lyme disease. Fecal transplant, budesonide, eight active prescriptions including gabapentin, clonazepam, and propranolol.

Her inflammatory markers were screaming. Kidney function swinging with the dehydration caused by the diarrhea caused by the colitis caused by the antibiotics. A cascade of iatrogenic damage stacked on a post-viral immune system that never stood down.

There is no clean ICD-10 code for what is wrong with Diane. Unspecified does not cut it. The patient is not making it up. She is suffering.

And she’s tried everything. Nattokinase, biofilm protocols, cranberry extract, digestive enzymes, a supplement shelf so tall it needed its own zip code. She’s listened to the gurus like McCullough and Gundry. Implemented their protocols. Some of it was smart. Most of it was noise. Nearly all of it was prescribed by well-meaning practitioners who were guessing. The system does not have a diagnostic pathway for a disease it cannot name.

Most readers are closer to Mike. Some are closer to Diane. The framework handles both. It just runs at different speeds.

18.3 The Three P's

All patients enter the clinic with one thing in common. They want answers to their questions. What is wrong with me? How do I get better? Do you have a cure for what's ailing me?

The Cockroach Diet has a process. It's easy to remember.

Precision, Priorities, and Principles.

Precision = measurement before intervention. We’ve discussed it ad nauseam. Comprehensive labs, genetic testing, sleep metrics, continuous glucose, imaging when warranted, family history, current medications, and a thorough History of Present Illness / Injury / Inquiry (HPI). Appendix B has the lab reference review. Appendix D maps the 22 underaddressed conditions they are attached to. Use them to guide your AI prompts. A patient who walks into the clinic with an organized health history is a force to be reckoned with. AI makes that homework easier than it has ever been. When you’re dialed in, so is your provider. Diagnostics without strategy is noise.

Priorities = triage. Triage is the order of operations. Our priorities are OPS-AF. Kids will get the acronym. You just need to remember the body cannot fix everything at once. Beneficial interventions in the wrong sequence often fail. Recall Stephen Covey's famous Habit 3. Put first things first. Crucial biological systems rarely receive a stepwise approach. These ones are often completely overlooked. You've got the Fabulous Five Fundamentals from Chapter 3. STARTED from Chapters 6 through 12. The Three Levels framework. This is the missing sequence. And the sequence is important.

Optimize mitochondria. Without ATP, nothing else works. Start Week 1. Never discontinue.

Perfect hormones. They tell mitochondria how to act. Week 2 forward, the moment labs come back. Raise to ideal physiological levels.

Strengthen the gut. Seventy percent of immune function, most of your serotonin, all of your hormone recycling. Weeks 4 to 6 allow time for judicious assessment.

Address injuries and illness. Chronic pain, smoldering infections, recurring symptoms. Wait until Week 7 or 8 to introduce advanced or exotic therapies. STARTED, supplements, mitochondria, hormones, and gut are coming online by then. Everything regenerates better now. Paying ten grand for stem cells on day one is foolish if the body is not primed for healing.

Fortify healthy lifespan. Week 8 to 12 and onward. Your favorite biohackers and influencers can enter the chat. Intermittent fasting, sauna, cold plunge, peptides, and regenerative cycles. Last because they amplify a foundation that already works.

The timing is guidance, not gospel. If you have debilitating sciatica, you are not waiting until Week 7 to start strengthening your core and working with a physical therapist. If you need antibiotics for acute Lyme no one tested for, take the antibiotics. Priorities keep the foundation at the forefront and the sequence in check. They address the necessary systems that are often skipped.

Principles = how you live inside the operating system. Most protocols fail because the patient leaves the clinic with a supplement bag and no operating manual for the other 165 hours of the week. Principles are the user’s manual and they are in every chapter. To consolidate a few, Appendix E showcases eleven recipes detailing, “What the Cockroach Taught Us.” These are principles. Protocols will always change. The principles do not.

No One Protocol Serves Everyone

110 Percent

Aim Small, Miss Small

The Action Research Loop

Three P's. One operating system. You got this.

18.4 The Toolbox

This is a diet book. So here is the diet part for review. Pay attention to the supplements we add here. They’re deficient in most. Our great-grandparents probably did not need a supplement stack. We do. You can start supplementation after your blood draw. Source highest quality and lab vetted.

18.5 The 12-Week Example

Two patients have been coached on the STARTED protocol. They are ready for the 12-week rollout.

What the chart proves. STARTED applies to everyone. So do the three P's. Two patients running at completely different speeds, both improving. That is the metaheuristic.

18.6 What Twelve Weeks Buys You

Mike, Week 12. Insulin 5.1. Testosterone 780 without TRT. The fix was embarrassingly simple. Boron, heavy compound lifts three times a week, eight hours of sleep in a cold dark room. Free and bioavailable testosterone climbed once SHBG stopped hoarding it. Down 22 pounds on a microdosed GLP-1. hs-CRP 0.6. Vitamin D 62. Brain fog gone. He signed up for a trail race in October. His wife says he is a different person. He is the same person with a body and a mind free of the shackles.

Diane, Week 12. Slower. Nonlinear. Measured in battles, not benchmarks. Gut stabilizing. IL-6 dropping. Kidney function recovered. 90% of patients require supplements. Hers were cut in half. Pharmaceuticals cut in half. Enrolled in a trial using ARA-290, stacking nerve-regeneration effects with HBOT. Weight continues to drop. She’s down 35 pounds on a microdose GLP-1. She feels better. She looks better. She still has a long way to go. The vertigo and the tinnitus may never fully resolve. We will keep trying. For the first time in two years, the interventions that remain are working with each other instead of against each other.

Mike and Diane are two patients on the same 12-week rollout. Their first cycle. The next one goes deeper. They progress. They learn the tools. They are motivated to keep moving forward.

Welcome to your new operating system. The protocols will change. The framework will not. The cockroach does not quit after one molt. Neither do you.

You are the research.

Why Level 1 is hard, and why the system wants you soft

## Chapter 19: The Enemy

**chapter_id**: `chapter-19-the-enemy`
**word_count**: 1109
**sections**: 5

- 19.1 Secret Ingredients
- 19.2 The Story of Kopi Luwak Coffee
- 19.3 Same Excrement, Different Packaging
- 19.4 The Seven Domains: SCAMMED.
- 19.5 The Counterattack

### Body

“In your race for power and glory, you forgot one small detail.” – Weird Science

We have met the enemy and he is comfortable. He doesn’t storm the gates. He cozies up with a softer couch, faster delivery, and a quiet buzz in your pocket or your head. He lives in your pans, your pantry, your prescriptions, and your morning coffee.

This raises the most basic question: Does The Cockroach Diet allow coffee?

Black coffee is not only permitted. It’s celebrated.

When you take a sip of coffee, caffeine molecules slip into adenosine receptors like skeleton keys, blocking tiredness signals. This triggers alertness mechanisms, including a release of cortisol from your adrenal glands and enhanced neural activity in regions like the prefrontal cortex. A targeted neurochemical intervention in your own brain.

When you wake up, hydrate. Then drink some coffee. It amplifies what water restores. Green tea is great too. If you do not need caffeine at all, you are very impressive.

But you could be missing out on some quality protein.

19.1 Secret Ingredients

The FDA permits up to 10% insect parts, including cockroaches, in your raw coffee beans. If you drink coffee daily, you may have swallowed more cockroaches than I have.

Here is what else is legally allowed in your groceries, according to the FDA’s own Defect Levels Handbook.

Chocolate: 60 insect fragments per 100 grams.

Peanut butter: 30 insect fragments or 1 rodent hair per 100 grams.

Wheat flour: 75 insect fragments per 50 grams.

Tomato products: 30 fly eggs or 2 maggots per 100 grams.

Canned mushrooms: 20 maggots per 100 grams.

Popcorn kernels: 2 rodent hairs per pound.

Ground spices: 400 insect fragments per 50 grams.

Fruit juice: 5 fly eggs or 1 maggot per 250 milliliters.

Dried fruit: 13 insect heads per 100 grams.

To avoid bugs in your beans, consider switching to the world’s most expensive coffee. It is called Kopi Luwak.

19.2 The Story of Kopi Luwak Coffee

In the 1830s the Dutch East India Company sailed into northern Sumatra and announced the land now belonged to coffee. With the polite backing of heavy artillery, the local farmers were welcome to stay on as forced labor. One rule: they were not allowed to drink the delicious bourgeois beverage themselves.

Forced labor was hard. Coffee was delicious. Rules are rules. If they wanted extra pep in their step the farmers had to get creative.

They noticed that a small, nocturnal, cat-like creature called the Asian palm civet loved coffee cherries. It wandered through plantations at night, ate only the ripest fruit, and after a brief digestive detour, left the beans behind. Intact. Stripped of bitterness. Enhanced by gut enzymes. The loophole.

The locals collected these fragrant offerings, gave them a quick rinse, roasted them, and brewed what was by all accounts, the smoothest coffee anyone had ever tasted. Technically, they were not harvesting the Dutch crop. They were cleaning up after wildlife. Win-win.

But the Dutch smelled what they were cooking. And because nothing delights a colonial tyrant more than turning someone else’s ingenuity into a profit center, civet coffee, Kopi Luwak, was promptly declared a rare delicacy.

Demand was immediate. Prices soared. Civets were trapped, caged, force-fed coffee cherries and the farmers who invented the drink were reduced to sifting through defecated beans. Today a pound of authentic Kopi Luwak runs $500 in a Tokyo department store. Most of the world’s supply comes from sad animals in wire cages.

Some things never change. They just get better marketing.

19.3 Same Excrement, Different Packaging

The Kopi Luwak parable is not about coffee. It is the operating manual for almost everything we call progress. Give any group enough power and distance from consequences, and ethics sneak out the back door.

The honest path is slow, small-scale, and rooted in relationships. The profitable path is fast, faceless, and willing to cage whatever, or whoever, stands in the way.

Consider a more popular example: cobalt. The miracle mineral that keeps your phone charged, your EV humming, and your solar panels virtuous. More than 70% of it comes from the Democratic Republic of Congo. Children as young as six crawl through hand-dug tunnels with no lights, no masks, and wages that would not buy a candy bar. They gamble collapsed lungs and broken spines, so your battery icon stays green a little longer. Amnesty International, Siddharth Kara, and the U.S. Department of Labor have documented all of it. The reports collect dust on shelves.

The enemy is subtle and slippery. He does not need to conquer you. He only needs you to stay comfortable.

The AI revolution will supercharge the system. Power, lust, and greed will scale with incredible economy of motion. New empires will rise on the same broken backs and unmarked graves. We will remain blissfully unaware.

We are not the victims. We create the demand. We fund the machine. We hold the power yet remain powerless. All of it to serve one enemy. The Code of Comfort.

19.4 The Seven Domains: SCAMMED.

When the axe came into the forest, the trees said: ‘The handle is one of us.” — Turkish proverb

A thousand years ago, comfort was the goal. Today, it’s a cancer.

S — Stagnation Swamp

Start here: The cure is the same as the decay. Slow and consistent. Reverse the curse. Pick one 90-day war. Cold showers every morning. Cook five nights a week. Zero alcohol. Build your glutes. Choose your battlefield and charge.

C — Cage of Convenience

Start here: Eat real food. Local and seasonal when you can. Treat every bite like molecular code, not chemical entertainment. Stop letting corporations cook for you.

A — Adversity Avoidance

Start here:

M — Media Mirage

Start here: Thirty days with zero “treat yourself” purchases. Unfollow anyone who makes you feel small. Question every headline before you share it. Talk to people in person. That void is where reality grows back.

M — Mediocrity Machine

Start here: Order your labs. Get STARTED. Run the 12-Week Rollout. Act before the system owns you.

E — Ecosystem of Entitlement

Start here: Plastic out. Glass and steel in. Filter your water. Stop treating your life like it is disposable. Same goes for the Goldilocks rock you ride.

D — Delusions of Adequacy

This is S-C-A-M-M-E-D. Seven leashes. Seven lies. Now you see them clearly. Knowing is not enough.

19.5 The Counterattack

Every system that profits from your comfort will punish your discipline. Every machine that demands compliance will try to crush what sets you free. Knowing is half the battle. Choosing wisely is the rest.

Time to slay.

— End of Chapter 19 —
