## APPENDIX D: The Field Manual: 22 Underaddressed Conditions

**chapter_id**: `appendix-d-the-field-manual-22-underaddressed-conditions`
**word_count**: 4356

### Body

Twenty-two conditions every adult should know how to recognize, test for, and address. This is not a textbook. It is a quick-reference field manual. Each card distills root cause, the diagnostic system blind spot, the labs to demand, the foundation moves, the advanced interventions, and the prevention strategy.

Hopefully you have none of these. Skim them anyway. Pattern recognition is the difference between catching something at week six and catching it at year six. Most readers will recognize themselves or someone they love in at least one card.

The hack: copy any card, paste it into the Roach Coach or any AI, and ask, "What additional tests or interventions should I consider?" Treat it like a tireless research assistant that does not bill by the hour.

A note on overlap: many of these conditions feed each other. Mold drives MCAS. MCAS drives gut dysfunction. Gut dysfunction drives autoimmune thyroid disease. The cards are organized by anatomy. The underlying biology rarely respects category lines. If you match three cards, that is not three diseases. It is one cascade with three faces. Treat upstream first.

Every protocol below should be discussed with a qualified clinician. The Cockroach Diet is for cockroach consideration only.

APPENDIX E

You have read the science. You have learned that you are not a bag of chemicals. You are controlled lightning. Now lock it down and fire it up. Get out there and live your best life ever. Remember who is sitting at the table. And what the cockroach taught us. Every diet book needs some delicious recipes so here you go.

One gag can save you from a lifetime of disease. That cockroach was not lunch. It was a PhD in visceral intelligence.

Modern processed food is a stealth cockroach in shiny packaging. Emulsifiers dissolve your mucosal lining. Seed oils flood Complex I with damaged electrons that leak as free radicals. Artificial sweeteners alter your microbiome within 48 hours. Your gut recoils. You override it because the packaging has a sunset on it.

Stop overriding it.

Practical Tips:

• Zonulin is the biomarker for intestinal permeability: test it. If elevated, your gut wall is leaking undigested proteins into your bloodstream, triggering systemic inflammation.

• BPC-157 (250–500 mcg daily, oral or subcutaneous) is the most studied peptide for gut lining repair. Gray market but backed by over 100 studies. KPV and L-glutamine are solid add-ons.

• Elimination diet for 30 days: remove gluten, dairy, seed oils, artificial sweeteners. Reintroduce one at a time. Let your gut vote.

• Molecular hydrogen water (1–2 tablets daily) may reduce gut oxidative stress at the mitochondrial level. Cheap. Maybe effective.

• Next time you see a processed snack, imagine it with antennae. Instant appetite killer.

Your gut feeling is not fringe. It is evolution screaming NO to anything that threatens electron flow. Honor it.

Recipe #2: Build a Bulletproof Biome

You’re going to eat bad snacks. The salty, fat, sweets are too sexy to resist. Strengthen the gut feeling.

Your gut bacteria are not passengers. They are cofactor factories. They synthesize B vitamins, vitamin K2, short-chain fatty acids, and neurotransmitters. They produce butyrate, the primary fuel for colonocytes, which directly supports mitochondrial function in the gut wall. A diverse microbiome does not just digest food. It manufactures the cofactors your electron transport chain requires and produces 90 percent of your body’s serotonin.

Dysbiosis, the collapse of microbial diversity, is linked to depression, Alzheimer’s, autoimmunity, obesity, and metabolic syndrome through the gut-brain axis. You cannot fix the electron transport chain if the organisms that supply its cofactors are dead.

Practical Tips:

• Test first: GI-MAP stool analysis gives you a species-level inventory of your microbiome, including pathogens, parasites, and inflammatory markers.

• Akkermansia muciniphila patches your gut lining. Lactobacillus reuteri modulates your amygdala and reduces anxiety. Bifidobacterium reduces systemic inflammation. Target by need, not by marketing.

• If wrecked: consider a targeted antimicrobial protocol (rifaximin for SIBO), then rebuild with strain-specific probiotics and prebiotic fiber.

• Polyphenols (berries, dark chocolate, green tea) are microbiome fertilizer. They are not antioxidants for you; they are antioxidants for your bacteria, which then produce the metabolites that protect your mitochondria.

• Laugh at people chugging kombucha while eating Doritos. Your biome knows the difference.

A diverse microbiome is not a wellness trend. It is the supply chain for your electron transport chain.

Recipe #3: Rewire the Roach Brain

The headless roach survives for a week. You do not.

The vagus nerve, the longest cranial nerve, running from brainstem to gut, is the master switch. High vagal tone means efficient parasympathetic recovery, lower inflammation, better heart rate variability, and stronger electron flow. Low vagal tone means your body never leaves the battlefield.

• HRV (heart rate variability) is your vagal tone biomarker. Track it daily with an Oura ring or Apple Watch. Rising HRV = improving autonomic resilience.

• Nasal breathing activates parasympathetic pathways and delivers endogenous nitric oxide to modulate mitochondrial respiration. Breathe through your nose. Always.

• Cold exposure (2–3 minutes cold shower or ice bath) triggers a norepinephrine surge of 200–300 percent that resets sympathetic tone and trains stress recovery.

• NAD+ IV or subcutaneous (100–250 mg weekly) directly fuels electron transport. Pair with methylated B12 for nervous system repair.

• Box breathing (4-4-4-4) stimulates the vagus nerve. Do it before meals to shift into rest-and-digest before you eat.

• Treat your nervous system like elite hardware. Do not run Windows 95 on a quantum computer.

Stress does not kill you. The inability to turn it off does.

Recipe #4: Start Early, Compound Like Interest

Waiting until you are broken is like fixing a roach infestation after the house is already crawling.

But here is the compound interest principle applied to biology: every intervention you start today prevents exponential degradation tomorrow. A 25-year-old who optimizes sleep, sunlight, and NAD+ will enter their fifties with mitochondrial capacity that their sedentary peers lost at 35. You are not anti-aging. You are pro-compounding.

Practical Tips:

• D3 + K2 daily (5,000 IU D3 with 200 mcg K2-MK7). Vitamin D is a mitochondrial signaling molecule, not just a bone vitamin. K2 directs calcium to bones, not arteries.

• Methylated B vitamins (methylfolate, methylcobalamin, P5P); structural components of electron carriers. If you have MTHFR variants, unmethylated Bs are useless to you.

• CoQ10 as ubiquinol (100–300 mg daily with fat). Crucial after 40 or on a statin.

• NMN or NR (250–500 mg daily) to support NAD+ salvage pathway. The electron transport chain starts with NAD+. Do not let it run dry.

• Annual bloodwork starting at 25. Fasting insulin, hsCRP, homocysteine, full thyroid, vitamin D, RBC magnesium. Build your baseline when you are healthy so you can see the decline before it becomes disease.

• Compound interest for cells: start at 20, thank yourself at 80.

You are not fighting aging. You are negotiating with entropy. Start early.

Recipe #5: Eat for Electrons, Move Like a Cockroach

Cockroaches eat to survive, not to Netflix-binge feelings.

Emotional eating is not a psychological problem. It is an electrical one. Sugar triggers a dopamine spike that temporarily boosts ATP through rapid glycolysis; the metabolic shortcut that bypasses the electron transport chain entirely. You feel better for twenty minutes. Then you crash, because glycolysis produces 2 ATP per glucose molecule while oxidative phosphorylation produces 36. You traded a power plant for a disposable battery. And you will crave another one in an hour.

Meanwhile, your mitochondria are demand-responsive. They do not maintain capacity you do not use. Sit in a chair for 8 hours and your body decommissions mitochondria in the muscles you are not using. The electrical grid shrinks. ATP production falls. You feel tired, not because you are overworked, but because you have fewer power plants than you did last month.

Fuel Tips:

• Prioritize electron-clean fuel: pastured eggs, wild-caught fish, grass-fed meat, organ meats (nature’s multivitamin), deeply colored vegetables.

• Avoid electron-dirty fuel: seed oils (damaged linoleic acid overwhelms Complex I), ultra-processed food (emulsifiers + colorants + preservatives = mitochondrial noise), excess sugar (reverse electron transfer at Complex I = free radical storm).

• Intermittent fasting (16:8 or 18:6) triggers autophagy: your cells’ recycling program. Damaged mitochondria get cleared and replaced. Factory reset for the electrical grid.

• GLP-1 agonists (tirzepatide, retatrutide) are not cheating. They reset hunger signaling. Use them if needed while you fix the underlying dysfunction. No shame.

• The cockroach eats drywall and its siblings and extracts energy with ruthless efficiency. That efficiency is the goal. The cannibalism is optional.

Movement Tips:

• 150 minutes per week minimum. This is the dose below which all-cause mortality curves steepen sharply.

• Three modalities: Zone 2 cardio (mitochondrial efficiency), HIIT (hormetic free radical burst → antioxidant upregulation), resistance training (mTOR → structural capacity). All three. Not one.

• PQQ (10–20 mg daily) stimulates mitochondrial biogenesis. The only compound known to trigger creation of entirely new mitochondria.

• Creatine monohydrate (5 - 10 g daily). Donates phosphate to recycle ATP. Crosses the blood-brain barrier. Improves cognition and may be neuroprotective. Not just for bodybuilders.

• TB-500 + BPC-157 for recovery (Wolverine stack). Gray market but extensively studied.

• Consistency compounds. Intensity impresses. Consistency wins.

If the electron transport chain is dialed, bad food is not as bad. If the chain is broken, good food cannot save you.

Recipe #6: Laugh at Life’s Absurdities

Humor turns poison into punchline.

Laughter is medicine, and a book you laugh through is a book you finish, remember, and act on. The cockroach dare was ridiculous. The science is not. Both can be true simultaneously.

Practical Tips:

• Daily laugh quota. Watch stand-up or try it writing your own minute for a Kill Tony bucket pull. Call the friend who makes you ugly-laugh. Schedule it like you schedule the gym.

• The difference between trauma and comedy is time and framing. Shorten the time.

• Senolytics (fisetin 500 mg, quercetin 500 mg, intermittent dosing) clear senescent ‘zombie cells’ that secrete inflammatory signals. Clearing the hecklers so the comedy can land.

• Thymosin Alpha-1 (1.6 mg subcutaneous, 2–3x/week) revives thymus function for T-cell production. Stay immune to the haters.

• Laugh often. Age slower.

A life without laughter is a mitochondria without CoQ10. Functional but dying.

Recipe #7: Cultivate Core Memories

We are not here forever. Make stories worth retelling when the streetlights come on.

Oxytocin, released during physical touch, deep conversation, and genuine connection, reduces cortisol, lowers inflammatory cytokines, improves wound healing, and directly supports mitochondrial function through reduction of oxidative stress. Loneliness, by contrast, increases all-cause mortality by 26 percent. It is more dangerous than obesity and equivalent to smoking 15 cigarettes a day. Social isolation is a mitochondrial toxin.

Practical Tips:

• One intentional memory per day. A conversation that goes deeper than logistics. A meal where phones are left in another room.

• Prioritize sleep for memory consolidation. Bad sleep = lost memories. You cannot forge core memories if your brain deletes them overnight.

• DSIP (Delta Sleep-Inducing Peptide, 100–300 mcg before bed) supports slow-wave sleep architecture for those who struggle with deep sleep phases. Epithalon and pinealon may work even better.

• Nurture people who knew you before you got serious. They will remind you who you are.

• Relationships are not a lifestyle add-on. They are a mitochondrial input. Budget time for them the way you budget for supplements.

Loneliness is a disease. Connection is the medicine.

Recipe #8: Fabricated Adversity Is a Cheat Code

The dare was accidental hormesis. Pick poisons you control and turn discomfort into superpower.

Practical Tips:

• Cold exposure: 2–3 minutes at 50–59°F. Triggers 200–300% norepinephrine increase, activates brown fat thermogenesis, trains mitochondrial uncoupling.

• Sauna: 20 minutes at 170–180°F, 3–4x/week. Heat shock proteins refold damaged proteins and protect mitochondrial membranes. All-cause mortality drops 40% in frequent sauna users.

• Fasting: 16–24 hours triggers autophagy. Controlled demolition of your worst power plants.

• Breath holds: Voluntary apnea trains HIF-1α upregulation. The cockroach survives without oxygen for 45 minutes. You are training the same adaptive pathway.

• Cycle stressors. Cold Monday, sauna Tuesday, fast Wednesday, heavy lift Thursday. Recover hard between. Hormesis without recovery is just damage.

Hard times often lead to the great moments.

Recipe #9: Seek One-Trial Lessons

One cockroach rewired me forever. When the streetlights come on, you will remember the dares, the moments, and trials that brought you home.

You can engineer these moments deliberately. Not by eating insects, we have established that was a poor decision, but by seeking experiences intense enough to rewrite neural pathways. First cold plunge. First 24-hour fast. First blood panel where you actually read the results. First conversation where you tell someone the truth instead of the comfortable version. These are neuroplastic events. And they compound.

Practical Tips:

• Lion’s Mane (500 mg–1 g daily). Stimulates nerve growth factor (NGF) and brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF). Makes your neurons more receptive to rewiring.

• Cerebrolysin (5–10 mL IM, cycled) for accelerated neuroplasticity. Used in post-stroke and TBI recovery. Extreme end of the toolkit but real.

• Journal after pivotal experiences. Writing forces your hippocampus to consolidate the event into long-term memory with deliberate framing. You choose what the lesson was, not your amygdala.

• Growth shocks are voluntary, time-limited, and processed. Trauma is involuntary, uncontrolled, and unresolved. You want the cold plunge, not the shipwreck.

• One dare per month. Something that scares you in a way you can control.

One trial. One lesson. One permanent upgrade to the neural code.

Recipe #10: You Are Not Fragile. You Never Were.

You are the genetic descendant of every organism that survived for 3.8 billion consecutive years. Not one of your ancestors failed before reproducing.

And somewhere along the way, someone convinced you that you are fragile. That you need a pill to sleep. A doctor’s note to exercise. An insurance company’s approval to understand your own blood work. A government agency’s permission to access a peptide that your own stomach produces naturally. You are not fragile. You are mismanaged.

Order your own labs. Read your own results. Fire the doctor who will not listen. Stock your own supplements. Run the GI-MAP, the DUTCH, the Wheat Zoomer. The protocols are in this book. The labs are available direct-to-consumer. You already have more tools than any patient in history. The only single point of failure left is you deciding not to use them.

One day, the streetlights will come on for us all. It will be time to go home. When that happens, you will not remember the comfortable days. You will remember the moments that forged you; the cockroach dares, the diagnoses that nearly broke you, the labs that finally told the truth, the people who fought beside you, and the protocols that put you back together when the system said there was nothing more it could do.

You are a cockroach in a world that keeps trying to step on you. Cockroaches do not go extinct.

Recipe #11: Protect the Signal

At the smallest level, you are not solid. You are probability. You are electrons moving through protein scaffolds, responding to light, interacting with fields you cannot see.

A headless cockroach taught us that. It survives because its electrical system stays coherent when everything else falls apart. Same goes for you. You have the STARTED protocol. You have the labs. You have the protocols. You have the Roach Coach.

All you need is the decision.

So here it is. The final recipe. The one the other ten were building toward.

Protect the signal of your life. The direction. The purpose. The thing that makes the maintenance worthwhile. Because biology does not resist entropy for no reason. It resists entropy because something in the architecture of existence insists on moving toward order, toward complexity, toward consciousness, toward you; sitting here, reading this, deciding what to do next. The cockroach does not know why it survives. It just does. You have the rare and terrible privilege of choosing why. Choose well. Start tomorrow morning. With the sunrise.

Long may he nourish us all.

APPENDIX F

The One-Day Reboot Checklist:

FOUR WEEKS OUT

NIGHT BEFORE

THE DAY

THE THREE SENTENCES

Why I have been stuck.

What I refuse to let my life become.

What I am becoming.

ONE YEAR LATER

Go to bed one person. Wake up different.

Dr. Elaine Marquez, CEO and the steady hand behind everything operational. Pamela, who keeps the money honest. Zlatin, who builds in code what I sketch on napkins. Daniel, who tracks every dollar. Jesse Natale and Summit Studios, who make it all look good.

Dr. Shariar Cohen, Dr. Sunil Kurup, and Dr. Barbara Krutchkoff, my IRB partners who believe that medicine should be practiced with courage. Do no harm but also help.

The patients who trusted protocols that no textbook endorsed. You were braver than most physicians. Your results are the evidence this book stands on.

The researchers cited in these endnotes, many of whom will never know their work ended up in a book about cockroaches. Science moves forward because people publish what they find, even when it is inconvenient.

And to the cockroach. Three hundred million years and counting. Long may you nourish us all.

## About the Author

### Body

M. Scott Mortensen, MD, MPAS, MLT, has spent 30 years in clinical medicine across every level of acuity. EMT at 19. Paramedic. Flight Medic. Fire Captain. Physician Assistant. Medical Doctor. He has practiced in emergency departments, remote wilderness settings, the back of helicopters, and structure fires.

He is the founder of Mortensen Medical and Complete Performance Center in Thousand Oaks, California, a concierge practice specializing in compounded hormones, peptide therapy, and regenerative medicine. He operates a nonprofit clinic serving veterans, first responders, and uninsured patients.

He is building AI SaaS automated workflows for patients and providers.

## Glossary

**chapter_id**: `glossary`
**word_count**: 5084

### Body

AFib. Atrial fibrillation. Irregular electrical chaos in the upper chambers of the heart. The most common sustained arrhythmia. Half of cases respond to lifestyle and biomarker work before any ablation suite gets involved.

AMP Framework. Adventure, Momentum, Purpose. The Level II compass for building a Noble Pursuit. Adventure pushes you outside your comfort zone. Momentum is small daily progress toward a productive goal. Purpose is creating something bigger than yourself. Three coordinates, one direction: up and out.

APOE4. A genetic variant of the apolipoprotein E gene. One copy increases Alzheimer's risk roughly threefold. Two copies, roughly twelvefold. A warning light, not a death sentence. The lifestyle response matters more than the genotype.

Arsenal, The. The collection of advanced tools in Level II: peptides, biologics, devices, and regenerative therapies used to optimize performance and recovery beyond what STARTED alone provides. Chapter 20 is the armory tour.

BDNF. Brain-Derived Neurotrophic Factor. The fertilizer for brain cells. Exercise raises it. Sleep deprivation crashes it. Several peptides (Cerebrolysin, Semax, Dihexa) chase its receptors.

BHRT. Bioidentical Hormone Replacement Therapy. Hormones molecularly identical to what your body made when it was younger. Compare with the synthetic variants that drove the Women's Health Initiative scare in 2002.

BOLT (Body Oxygen Level Test). A measure of CO2 tolerance developed by Patrick McKeown from the Buteyko method. After a normal breath in and out, pinch your nose and time the seconds until the first definite urge to breathe. Under 20 indicates chronic overbreathing. 20–30 average. 30–40 good. Above 40 is elite respiratory efficiency, correlating with calm nervous system tone, low resting respiratory rate, and superior oxygen delivery to tissues. Free to test. Embarrassing to fail.

BPC-157. Body Protection Compound 157. A 15-amino-acid fragment of a protein in human gastric juice. The Swiss Army knife of peptides. Heals tendons, ligaments, gut lining, and most things that are broken below the neck. The gray-market crown jewel.

Cardiolipin. A phospholipid that lives only in mitochondrial inner membranes. Damaged cardiolipin equals damaged mitochondria. SS-31 (Elamipretide) stabilizes it.

CAC Score. Coronary Artery Calcium score from a low-dose chest CT. Quantifies calcified atherosclerotic plaque. Zero means roughly one percent ten-year cardiac event risk. Above 300 puts you in the same risk tier as someone who has already had a heart attack. Cheap. Powerful. Underordered.

CIRS. Chronic Inflammatory Response Syndrome. The clinical picture that emerges when biotoxins (usually mold) accumulate in genetically susceptible people who cannot clear them. Multi-system, multi-symptom, missed by most doctors.

Cockroach Brain. The unaugmented, fully biological, non-implanted, non-uploaded human nervous system. The default operating system. Increasingly counter-cultural.

Cockroach Diet, The. Not a diet. A regenerative framework for staying alive, alert, and useful as biotech, food systems, and digital life collectively try to obsolete the human body. The book you are reading. Also a cockroach.

Code of Comfort. The invisible set of habits, routines, and avoidance patterns that keep you safe but stagnant. The thing Level I asks you to dismantle. Most people defend it for forty years and then wonder why they never lived.

Cosmic Calling. The Level III destination. The deepest expression of purpose, meaning, and contribution a human can pursue. Yours may not look like anyone else's. That is the point.

Create More Than You Consume. The Level II mandate. Output exceeds input. Build something. Make something. Plant something. Write something. The opposite of doomscrolling.

CRH. Corticotropin-Releasing Hormone. The hypothalamic signal that kicks off the cortisol cascade. Mast cells have CRH receptors, which is why stress triggers MCAS flares directly.

DEXA. Dual-energy X-ray Absorptiometry. The gold-standard bone density scan. Also measures body composition. Cheap, fast, surprisingly informative.

DHEA. Dehydroepiandrosterone. The most abundant steroid hormone in the human body and a precursor to testosterone and estradiol. Crashes with age. Easy to replace.

DMT. Dimethyltryptamine. An endogenous tryptamine released in trace amounts by the human brain, with concentrations that spike in dying rats. The proposed neurochemistry behind some near-death experiences. The same molecule indigenous Amazonians brewed into ayahuasca three thousand years before Big Pharma noticed.

DUTCH. Dried Urine Test for Comprehensive Hormones. A four-sample diurnal cortisol curve plus sex-hormone metabolites. The functional medicine workhorse for adrenal and hormonal work-ups.

EAT-Lancet. A 2019 dietary recommendation framework promoting near-vegetarian eating to "save the planet." Funded by an ideological foundation. Cited frequently by people who have never worked a clinic full of B12-deficient vegans.

Fabricated Adversity. Deliberately choosing discomfort. Cold exposure, fasting, intense exercise, etc. Build resilience before life forces it on you. The cockroach does not wait for hardship. It trains for it.

Flexner Report. A 1910 Carnegie Foundation report on American medical education that crowned allopathic medicine, defunded homeopathy and naturopathy, and consolidated pharmaceutical-aligned medical schools. The starting gun for the modern medical-industrial complex.

Frameshifting. A ribosomal slip during protein translation that scrambles the reading frame. Documented in N¹-methylpseudouridine-modified mRNA. Either no big deal or quite a big deal, depending on who you read.

GBS. Guillain-Barré Syndrome. Acute autoimmune attack on peripheral nerves. Often post-viral or post-vaccine. The Miller Fisher variant adds cranial nerve involvement and ataxia. The author's N=1 case study lives in Chapter 21.

GI-MAP. Gastrointestinal Microbial Assay Plus. PCR-based comprehensive stool analysis. The single most important gut test in functional medicine. Identifies pathogens, dysbiosis, inflammation, and digestive function markers that standard ova-and-parasite exams miss seventy percent of the time.

GLP-1. Glucagon-Like Peptide 1. The hormone Ozempic mimics. Slows gastric emptying, signals satiety, lowers blood glucose. Up to forty percent of weight lost on semaglutide alone is lean mass. Scaffolding, not crutch.

GHK-Cu. A copper-binding tripeptide isolated from human plasma in 1973. Stimulates collagen synthesis and wound healing. Fifty years of data and still considered "experimental" by people who do not read.

HBOT. Hyperbaric Oxygen Therapy. Pressurized oxygen at 1.5 to 2.4 atmospheres absolute. Drives oxygen into hypoxic tissue, mobilizes stem cells, and accelerates neurological recovery. Twenty to forty sessions for serious work.

HLA-DR. Human Leukocyte Antigen, class II. The immune system's molecular ID badge. About a quarter of the population carries HLA-DR genotypes that cannot clear biotoxins, which is why some families get mold-sick and others walk through the same building untouched.

HPA Axis. Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal axis. The stress response highway. Chronic stress dysregulates it. Cortisol output flattens. Wired but tired.

hs-CRP. High-sensitivity C-Reactive Protein. The cheap inflammation marker. Above 1 means inflammation. Above 3 means a lot of it. The number cardiologists should be chasing instead of LDL.

Innate Repair Receptor. A heterodimeric receptor formed from the erythropoietin receptor and the beta common receptor. The target of ARA-290 (Cibinetide). Activated, it suppresses inflammation and supports tissue repair without raising hemoglobin.

KLOW. A compounded peptide blend: KPV + L-glutamine + GHK-Cu + TB-500 + BPC-157. The "everything stack" for repair, anti-inflammatory work, and skin regeneration. The Swiss Army knife of Swiss Army knives.

KPV. A tripeptide fragment of alpha-MSH (lysine-proline-valine). Anti-inflammatory in the gut and on the skin without immunosuppression. Stacks with BPC-157 for leaky gut and skin barrier issues.

Leap of Faith. The Level III transition from optimized body and purposeful mind to the question of ultimate meaning. The decision to believe that life is not accidental and to act accordingly.

Level I: Healthy. STARTED protocol + Fabricated Adversity + Code of Comfort dismantling. Fix the body. Build the foundation.

Level II: Wealthy. Noble Pursuit + AMP Framework + Create More Than You Consume. Build adventure, momentum, and purpose.

Level III: Wise. Leap of Faith + Cosmic Calling. Confront the biggest questions. Decide what you believe. Live accordingly.

Levothyroxine. Synthetic T4. Third or fourth most prescribed drug in the United States. The default thyroid prescription. Works fine for good T4-to-T3 converters. Leaves poor converters cold and exhausted and confused about why their TSH is "normal."

Long COVID. The post-acute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection. Persistent cytokine dysregulation, mitochondrial dysfunction, exercise intolerance, brain fog, autonomic chaos. ICD-10 code U09.9, finalized two years after the first cases were described.

Lp(a). Lipoprotein (a). A genetically determined cardiovascular risk factor that statins do not move. Test once in your life. If high, plan accordingly. PCSK9 inhibitors lower it.

MACI. Matrix-induced Autologous Chondrocyte Implantation. FDA-approved in 2016. Cartilage cells harvested, grown on a collagen membrane, surgically reinstalled. Roughly 85 percent implant survival. Real regenerative medicine that already exists.

MCAS. Mast Cell Activation Syndrome. Mast cells degranulate to triggers that shouldn't provoke them: foods, fragrances, temperature, exercise. Flushing, hives, brain fog, GI distress, tachycardia. Frequently downstream of mold, Lyme, or gut barrier failure. Patient is usually told it is anxiety.

MIND Act. Management of Individuals' Neural Data Act. U.S. Senate bill from September 2025 directing the FTC to examine commercial use of neural data captured by brain-computer interfaces. Lawmakers are already worried.

Molt, The. Chapter 11's metaphor for exercise. The cockroach must shed its exoskeleton to grow. You must shed your sedentary nervous system to add muscle. Same principle.

Mortensen Medical. The author's nonprofit clinic in Thousand Oaks, California. The mothership.

MSC. Mesenchymal Stem Cells. Multipotent adult stem cells used in regenerative orthopedics, autoimmune disease, and cardiovascular repair. The Floor 3 heavy equipment.

NAD+. Nicotinamide Adenine Dinucleotide. The cellular energy currency. Declines roughly fifty percent between ages 40 and 60. CD38 (which goes up with inflammation) eats it. Replacing it is one of the most consistent feel-better interventions in this book.

NDE. Near-Death Experience. The transcendent or autobiographical phenomena reported by a subset of cardiac arrest survivors. The AWARE-II study documented memories consistent with consciousness during CPR. Either fascinating evidence or a clue. Pick your worldview.

N¹-methylpseudouridine. The modified RNA base used in the Pfizer-BioNTech and Moderna mRNA COVID-19 vaccines to stabilize the molecule and reduce immune recognition. Implicated in ribosomal frameshifting in 2023. See Frameshifting above.

Noble Pursuit. A mission worth waking up for. Not a hobby. Not a side hustle. A creative endeavor that outlasts your heartbeat and serves someone other than yourself.

OPS-AF. Priorities. Optimize mitochondria. Perfect hormones. Strengthen the gut. Address injuries and illness. Fortify healthy lifespan.

Oxidized LDL. Low-density lipoprotein that has been chemically modified by oxidative stress. Native LDL is a passenger. Oxidized LDL is the troublemaker. Seed oils accelerate the oxidation.

PA-C. Physician Assistant, Certified. The author's primary clinical credential. The reason any of the clinical claims in this book come from a clinician who has written prescriptions.

Pamela Reynolds Case. The 1991 standstill craniotomy where a patient with no measurable brain activity reported veridical perceptions of the operating room. The single most famous near-death experience case in the medical literature.

Pancreatic Elastase. A pancreatic enzyme measured in stool. Below 200 means pancreatic insufficiency. Below 100 means severe. A claim of "IBS" without an elastase result is not a diagnosis. It is a shrug.

Penrose-Hameroff (Orch-OR). Orchestrated Objective Reduction. The Roger Penrose and Stuart Hameroff hypothesis that consciousness arises from quantum computations in neuronal microtubules. The Wellesley epothilone-B experiments support a piece of it. Mainstream neuroscience is still embarrassed about it.

Peptide Bioregulator. A short peptide that signals an organ to operate more like its younger version. Khavinson's life work. Epithalon, Pinealon, and the rest of the Russian-pedigree lineup.

PMA. Private Membership Association. A legal structure that allows members to access services and information outside the public commercial sphere. The cockroach's preferred legal home.

PRP. Platelet-Rich Plasma. Your own blood, spun down and concentrated, then re-injected. Used in orthopedics, hair restoration, and aesthetic medicine. Works. Meta-analyses agree.

Quantum Symbiosis. The author's framework: consciousness and physical reality exist in a symbiotic relationship. Consciousness uses matter to experience itself. Matter uses consciousness to organize itself against entropy. Neither is primary. Both are eternal expressions of the same underlying reality.

Reverse T3. The metabolic brake pedal. An inactive thyroid hormone that competes with active T3 at receptor sites. Elevated under inflammation, stress, illness. Endocrinologists rarely check it. Functional medicine doctors live by it.

ROACH. La Résistance Organique Against Computerized Humans. The movement for people who intend to remain organic, autonomous, and unchipped in an age of neural implants and brain-computer interfaces. French flair optional. Off-grid compound preferred.

Roach Coach, The. The book's AI sidekick. Paste any card, any chapter, any lab result, and ask the next question.

Rudman Study, The. Daniel Rudman's 1990 NEJM paper showing 4.3 percent lean mass gain and 13.1 percent fat loss in six months on growth hormone in men over 60. The paper that started the modern GH-replacement conversation.

SHBG. Sex Hormone Binding Globulin. The protein that grabs onto testosterone and estradiol and renders them inactive. High SHBG equals low free hormone equals symptoms despite "normal" totals.

SIBO. Small Intestinal Bacterial Overgrowth. Bacteria colonizing the small intestine where they don't belong. Bloating, gas, food intolerance. Treatable with rifaximin or herbal antimicrobials.

SS-31 (Elamipretide). A cardiolipin-stabilizing peptide. FDA-approved September 2025 for Barth syndrome under the brand name Forzinity. The first mitochondria-targeted drug ever approved.

STARTED. The Level I protocol. Sunlight. Temperance. Air. Rest. Trust. Exercise. Diet. Seven foundations that must be in place before any advanced intervention matters. If your STARTED is shaky, your peptides are decoration.

STEP-1. The pivotal phase 3 trial of semaglutide 2.4 mg published in 2021 NEJM. Roughly 15 percent body weight loss at 68 weeks. The trial that triggered the GLP-1 cultural moment.

Survivability Simulation. Chapter 13. A sealed-room thought experiment that forces the reader to confront what happens when every modern convenience is stripped away. The diagnostic tool for how fragile your current life actually is.

TB-500. Thymosin Beta-4 fragment 1-4. Promotes cell migration, angiogenesis, and systemic tissue repair. Pairs with BPC-157 for the Wolverine Protocol.

Thymosin Alpha-1. A thymus-derived peptide that modulates T-cell activity. Approved in 35+ countries for hepatitis B and C. Used off-label for chronic infection, immune dysfunction, and post-viral syndromes. Powerful enough to flare autoimmunity if used carelessly.

TPO Antibodies. Thyroid Peroxidase antibodies. The marker for Hashimoto's autoimmune thyroiditis. Selenium 200 mcg daily reduces them by 20 to 40 percent in clinical trials.

TRIIM Trial. Thymus Regeneration, Immunorestoration, and Insulin Mitigation. A 2019 trial showing measurable thymic regrowth and a 2.5-year reversal of epigenetic age using growth hormone plus DHEA plus metformin. The first human evidence that biological age can move backwards.

TSH. Thyroid-Stimulating Hormone. The pituitary's message to the thyroid. The number primary care doctors check and stop at. Necessary but not sufficient.

U09.9. The ICD-10-CM code for "Post-COVID-19 condition, unspecified." Finalized October 2021, almost two years after the first long-COVID cases were described. The diagnostic system is slow.

VO2 max. The maximum rate of oxygen uptake during exhaustive exercise. The single strongest predictor of all-cause mortality. Trainable at any age. Underrated by most physicians, worshipped by exercise physiologists.

WADA. World Anti-Doping Agency. The international body that maintains the Prohibited List for elite athletic competition. Banned BPC-157 in January 2022, which tells you something about how well it works.

Wolverine Protocol, The. BPC-157 plus TB-500. Daily SubQ injections, four to six weeks on, time off. For injury recovery, post-surgical healing, or training through hard physical work. Named for what your body feels like it can do at the end of a cycle.

APPENDIX D: Top 22 Underaddressed Conditions

Levitskaya, N. G., Sebentsova, E. A., Andreeva, L. A., et al. (2008). The neuroprotective effects of Semax in conditions modeling clinical applications. Doklady Biological Sciences, 423, 385–388. Semax is a synthetic heptapeptide analog of ACTH(4-10) that enhances BDNF and NGF expression in the rat hippocampus and basal forebrain. Intranasal administration has demonstrated neuroprotective, nootropic, and anxiolytic effects across multiple Russian clinical trials, including acute ischemic stroke and traumatic brain injury.

CMS. (2023). Lecanemab (Leqembi) coverage determination. List price $26,500/year. FDA traditional approval July 2023 for early Alzheimer's with confirmed amyloid pathology. Aducanumab (Aduhelm) withdrawn 2024. Efficacy of amyloid-targeting therapies remains debated.

American Thyroid Association. (2024). General information/press room. Approximately 20 million Americans have some form of thyroid disease; Hashimoto's thyroiditis affects ~14 million. Up to 60% of those with thyroid disease are unaware of their condition. Leading cause of hypothyroidism in iodine-sufficient countries.

ClinCalc DrugStats Database. (2024). Levothyroxine drug usage statistics. Third most prescribed medication in the U.S. in 2023 with over 80 million prescriptions. Fourth by total patients. Over 100 million annual prescriptions at peak.

Toulis, K. A., Anastasilakis, A. D., Tzellos, T. G., et al. (2010). Selenium supplementation in the treatment of Hashimoto's thyroiditis: A systematic review and meta-analysis. Thyroid, 20(10), 1163–1173. Selenium 200 mcg/day reduced TPO antibody levels by 20–40% across multiple RCTs. Mechanism: selenium is a cofactor for glutathione peroxidase and deiodinase enzymes critical to thyroid hormone metabolism.

Chandrasekhar, K., Kapoor, J., & Anishetty, S. (2012). A prospective, randomized double-blind, placebo-controlled study of safety and efficacy of a high-concentration full-spectrum extract of Ashwagandha root in reducing stress and anxiety in adults. Indian Journal of Psychological Medicine, 34(3), 255–262. 64 subjects, 60 days. KSM-66 Ashwagandha 600 mg/day reduced serum cortisol by 27.9% (commonly rounded to ~30%) vs. placebo. Significant reductions in all stress-assessment scales.

Chugh, S. S., Havmoeller, R., Narayanan, K., et al. (2014). Worldwide epidemiology of atrial fibrillation: A Global Burden of Disease 2010 Study. Circulation, 129(8), 837–847. Estimated 33.5 million people worldwide affected by atrial fibrillation.

Day, J. D., Bunch, T. J., & LaPlante, M. (2021). The AFib Cure: Get Off Your Medications, Take Control of Your Health, and Add Years to Your Life. BenBella Books. Combined 53 years of electrophysiology practice. Central thesis: biomarker and lifestyle optimization may put half of AFib cases into remission without drugs or procedures. AFib strikes 1 in 4 American adults. Modifiable triggers: alcohol, sleep apnea, obesity, excessive endurance exercise, electrolyte imbalances, systemic inflammation.

Gami, A. S., Hodge, D. O., Herges, R. M., et al. (2007). Obstructive sleep apnea, obesity, and the risk of incident atrial fibrillation. Journal of the American College of Cardiology, 49(5), 565–571. Untreated OSA associated with ~4× increased AFib risk.

Wang, T. J., Parise, H., Levy, D., et al. (2004). Obesity and the risk of new-onset atrial fibrillation. JAMA, 292(20), 2471–2477. Each 5-unit BMI increase: 29% increased AFib risk. Framingham Heart Study.

Abdulla, J., & Nielsen, J. R. (2009). Is the risk of atrial fibrillation higher in athletes than in the general population? A systematic review and meta-analysis. Europace, 11(9), 1156–1159. Endurance athletes: ~5× AFib risk vs. non-athletes.

Ravnskov, U., Diamond, D. M., Hama, R., et al. (2016). Lack of an association or an inverse association between low-density-lipoprotein cholesterol and mortality in the elderly: A systematic review. BMJ Open, 6(6), e010401. 19 cohort studies, 30 cohorts, 68,094 individuals aged ≥60. In 92% of participants, higher LDL-C was inversely associated with all-cause mortality (statistically significant in 14 of 16 cohorts). No study found higher LDL-C increased mortality. Note: study has been criticized for methodological limitations (CEBM Oxford post-publication review); most included cohorts did not adjust for HDL-C. The finding is provocative and legitimate but not consensus.

Oster, M. E., Shay, D. K., Su, J. R., et al. (2022). Myocarditis cases reported after mRNA-based COVID-19 vaccination in the US from December 2020 to August 2021. JAMA, 327(4), 331–340. Post-vaccine myocarditis incidence in males aged 16–24: approximately 1 in 5,000 to 1 in 10,000 after the second mRNA dose, depending on age and product. Most cases mild and self-limiting. Acknowledged by CDC, FDA, and international health agencies.

Perin, E. C., Borow, K. M., Henry, T. D., et al. (2023). Randomized trial of targeted transendocardial mesenchymal precursor cell therapy in patients with heart failure: The DREAM-HF trial. Circulation Research, 132(3), 381–397. 565 patients, 51 sites. 65% reduction in non-fatal MI and stroke. Patients with elevated inflammatory markers: 80% reduction in cardiac death. Largest stem cell trial in heart failure to date.

Raghu, G., Remy-Jardin, M., Richeldi, L., et al. (2022). Idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (an update) and progressive pulmonary fibrosis in adults: An official ATS/ERS/JRS/ALAT clinical practice guideline. American Journal of Respiratory and Critical Care Medicine, 205(9), e18–e47. ~130,000 Americans affected. Median survival 3–5 years from diagnosis. ~50,000 new cases/year. FDA-approved antifibrotics (pirfenidone, nintedanib) slow decline ~50% but do not reverse fibrosis.

Ma, Q., Ma, Y., Dai, X., et al. (2018). Regeneration of functional alveoli by adult human SOX9+ airway basal cell transplantation. Protein & Cell, 9(3), 267–282. Tongji University. P63+/SOX9+ lung progenitor cells transplanted into COPD patients. Imaging showed regeneration of damaged lung tissue. First demonstration of functional alveolar regeneration from adult human basal cells in a clinical setting.

Weitzberg, E., & Lundberg, J. O. (2002). Humming greatly increases nasal nitric oxide. American Journal of Respiratory and Critical Care Medicine, 166(2), 144–145. Humming increased nasal NO levels 15-fold compared to quiet exhalation.

Guntur, V. P., Nemkov, T., de Boer, E., et al. (2022). Signatures of mitochondrial dysfunction and impaired fatty acid metabolism in plasma of patients with post-acute sequelae of COVID-19 (PASC). Metabolites, 12(11), 1026. + Ajaz, S., McPhail, M. J., Singh, K. K., et al. (2021). Mitochondrial metabolic manipulation by SARS-CoV-2 in peripheral blood mononuclear cells of patients with COVID-19. American Journal of Physiology–Cell Physiology, 320(1), C57–C65. Multiple studies report 30–90% of long-COVID patients show measurable mitochondrial metabolic dysfunction. 2025 Frontiers in Immunology review consolidated these findings.

Massudi, H., Grant, R., Braidy, N., et al. (2012). Age-associated changes in oxidative stress and NAD+ metabolism in human tissue. PLoS ONE, 7(7), e42357. NAD+ levels decline approximately 50% between ages 40 and 60 in human skin and brain tissue. Decline correlates with increased oxidative damage and reduced mitochondrial function.

Cordero, M. D., Alcocer-Gómez, E., de Miguel, M., et al. (2013). Can coenzyme Q10 improve clinical and molecular parameters in fibromyalgia? Antioxidants & Redox Signaling, 19(12), 1356–1361. Fibromyalgia patients showed CoQ10 levels 40–50% below normal. RCT: 300 mg/day CoQ10 for 40 days reduced pain and fatigue by >50% and improved mitochondrial bioenergetics markers.

Fernández-Garza, L. E., Guillen-Silva, F., Sotelo-Ibarra, M. A., Domínguez-Mendoza, A., Barrera-Barrera, S. A., & Barrera-Saldaña, H. A. (2025). Growth hormone and aging: A clinical review. Frontiers in Aging, 6, 1549453.

Rudman, D., Feller, A. G., Nagraj, H. S., Gergans, G. A., Lalitha, P. Y., Goldberg, A. F., Schlenker, R. A., Cohn, L., Rudman, I. W., & Mattson, D. E. (1990). Effects of human growth hormone in men over 60 years old. New England Journal of Medicine, 323(1), 1-6.

Welle, S., Thornton, C., Statt, M., & McHenry, B. (1996). Growth hormone increases muscle mass and strength but does not rejuvenate myofibrillar protein synthesis in healthy subjects over 60 years old. Journal of Clinical Endocrinology and Metabolism, 81(9), 3239-3243. Sathiavageeswaran, M., Burman, P., Lawrence, D., Harris, A. G., Falleti, M. G., Maruff, P., & Wass, J. (2007). Effects of GH on cognitive function in elderly patients with adult-onset GH deficiency. European Journal of Endocrinology, 156(4), 439-447.

Kingsberg, S. A., Clayton, A. H., Portman, D., Williams, L. A., Krop, J., Jordan, R., Lucas, J., & Simon, J. A. (2019). Bremelanotide for the treatment of hypoactive sexual desire disorder: Two randomized phase 3 trials. Obstetrics and Gynecology, 134(5), 899-908.

Kosfeld, M., Heinrichs, M., Zak, P. J., Fischbacher, U., & Fehr, E. (2005). Oxytocin increases trust in humans. Nature, 435(7042), 673-676.

Younossi, Z. M., Koenig, A. B., Abdelatif, D., et al. (2016). Global epidemiology of nonalcoholic fatty liver disease: Meta-analytic assessment of prevalence, incidence, and outcomes. Hepatology, 64(1), 73–84. Global NAFLD prevalence ~25%; U.S. estimates range from 30–40% of adults. Prevalence increases with obesity, type 2 diabetes, and metabolic syndrome.

Wong, R. J., Aguilar, M., Cheung, R., et al. (2015). Nonalcoholic steatohepatitis is the second leading etiology of liver disease among adults awaiting liver transplantation in the United States. Gastroenterology, 148(3), 547–555. NASH was the fastest-growing indication for liver transplant and is projected to become the leading indication within the coming decade, surpassing hepatitis C and alcoholic liver disease.

Kennedy, O. J., Roderick, P., Buchanan, R., et al. (2017). Coffee, including caffeinated and decaffeinated coffee, and the risk of hepatocellular carcinoma: A systematic review and dose-response meta-analysis. BMJ Open, 7(5), e013739. + Poole, R., Kennedy, O. J., Roderick, P., et al. (2017). Coffee consumption and health: Umbrella review of meta-analyses of multiple health outcomes. BMJ, 359, j5024. 2–4 cups/day associated with lower risk of liver fibrosis, cirrhosis, and hepatocellular carcinoma across multiple meta-analyses. One of the most consistently hepatoprotective dietary exposures in the literature.

Stinton, L. M., & Shaffer, E. A. (2012). Epidemiology of gallbladder disease: Cholelithiasis and cancer. Gut and Liver, 6(2), 172–187. Approximately 750,000 cholecystectomies performed annually in the U.S., making it one of the most common elective surgical procedures. Gallstone prevalence 10–15% of the adult population. Risk factors include obesity, rapid weight loss, female sex, and Western diet.

Mudarri, D., & Fisk, W. J. (2007). Public health and economic impact of dampness and mold. Indoor Air, 17(3), 226–235. Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory. Estimated 50% of U.S. buildings have dampness or water damage conditions conducive to mold growth. Associated with 21% of asthma cases in the U.S., representing 4.6 million cases and an estimated annual cost of $3.5 billion.

Ang, C. W., Notermans, D. W., Hommes, M., Simoons-Smit, A. M., & Herremans, T. (2011). Large differences between test strategies for the detection of anti-Borrelia antibodies are revealed by comparing eight ELISAs and five immunoblots. European Journal of Clinical Microbiology & Infectious Diseases, 30(8), 1027–1032. + Stricker, R. B., & Johnson, L. (2011). Lyme disease: The next decade. Infection and Drug Resistance, 4, 1–9. Standard two-tier testing (ELISA followed by Western blot) has reported sensitivity as low as 40–60% in early and late Lyme disease, depending on stage and laboratory methodology.

Theoharides, T. C., et al. (2012). Mast cells and inflammation. Biochimica et Biophysica Acta, 1822(1), 21–33. Mast cells express corticotropin-releasing hormone (CRH) receptors; CRH directly stimulates mast cell degranulation and mediator release, providing a mechanistic link between psychological stress and mast cell activation.

Savioli, L., Bundy, D., & Tomkins, A. (1992). Intestinal parasitic infections: a soluble public health problem. Transactions of the Royal Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene, 86(4), 353–354. At least 30% of the world population is estimated to carry intestinal parasites.

Stensvold, C. R., & Clark, C. G. (2016). Current status of Blastocystis: a personal view. Parasitology International, 65(6), 763–771. Up to 15% of stool specimens submitted for ova and parasite examination in the United States test positive for Blastocystis. Prevalence is significantly higher in symptomatic GI populations. Global prevalence ranges from 2% to over 80% depending on geography and immune status.

CDC Neglected Parasitic Infections (NPIs). The CDC has designated five parasitic infections as U.S. public health priorities based on number of people infected, severity of illness, and availability of treatment: Chagas disease (>300,000 infected), cysticercosis (≥1,000 hospitalizations/year), toxocariasis (14% seropositive), toxoplasmosis (>60 million chronically infected), and trichomoniasis (1.1 million new infections/year).

CDC (2014); World Health Organization taeniasis/cysticercosis fact sheet. At least 1,000 people are hospitalized with neurocysticercosis annually in the United States. WHO estimates 2.56 to 8.30 million people globally live with neurocysticercosis. In endemic regions, neurocysticercosis is responsible for approximately 30% of all epilepsy cases.

Song, B., et al. (2022). Fenbendazole acts as a moderate microtubule destabilizing agent and causes cancer cell death by modulating multiple cellular pathways. Scientific Reports. Preclinical studies demonstrate anti-cancer effects through p53 activation, GLUT1 transporter inhibition, glucose uptake reduction, and microtubule disruption. Not FDA-approved for human use. No completed human clinical trials. Low toxicity profile in animal studies. Off-label use is growing but should be discussed with a provider.

Canavan, C., West, J., & Card, T. (2014). The epidemiology of irritable bowel syndrome. Clinical Epidemiology, 6, 71–80. IBS affects an estimated 10–15% of the global population. It is the most common diagnosis in gastroenterology, accounting for up to 40% of all referrals.

Chedid, V., Dhalla, S., Clarke, J. O., et al. (2014). Herbal therapy is equivalent to rifaximin for the treatment of small intestinal bacterial overgrowth. Global Advances in Health and Medicine, 3(3), 16–24. In a retrospective analysis, herbal therapies (including allicin, berberine, and oregano oil formulations) demonstrated a 46% response rate versus 34% for rifaximin in SIBO patients. Herbal therapy was non-inferior and, in some analyses, statistically superior.

EAU Guidelines on Urolithiasis (2024); Ziemba, J. B., et al. (2017). Journal of Urology. Kidney stone recurrence rates reach approximately 50% at 5 years and 80–90% at 10 years. Despite EAU and AUA recommendations, only 7–16% of stone formers undergo comprehensive 24-hour urine metabolic evaluation. In one population-based study of 28,836 high-risk patients, only 7.4% received metabolic workup.

De Souza, M. J., Strock, N., Williams, N. I., et al. (2022). Prunes preserve hip bone mineral density in a 12-month randomized controlled trial in postmenopausal women: the Prune Study. American Journal of Clinical Nutrition, 116(4), 883–893. In 235 postmenopausal women, 50g of prunes daily (5–6 prunes) prevented loss of total hip BMD at 6 and 12 months. Control group lost significant bone mass. The 50g dose showed 90% compliance and only 15% dropout, suggesting clinical feasibility as a nonpharmacologic strategy.

Vuolteenaho, K., Moilanen, T., & Moilanen, E. (2008). Non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drugs, cyclooxygenase-2 and the bone healing process. Basic & Clinical Pharmacology & Toxicology, 102(1), 10–14. Multiple studies demonstrate that chronic NSAID use inhibits proteoglycan synthesis in cartilage and may accelerate joint degeneration. Ibuprofen and indomethacin have both been associated with increased cartilage loss on imaging in OA patients compared to placebo in longitudinal studies.

FDA (2016). MACI (autologous cultured chondrocytes on porcine collagen membrane) approved for the repair of symptomatic, single or multiple full-thickness cartilage defects of the knee in adults. Vericel Corporation. In the SUMMIT trial, MACI demonstrated greater pain relief and functional improvement versus microfracture, with benefits maintained at 5-year follow-up. Approximately 85% of implants survive and integrate with surrounding cartilage.

Tuthill, C., et al. (2000). Thymosin alpha-1: past clinical experience and future promise. Annals of the New York Academy of Sciences, 917, 500–507. Thymosin alpha-1 modulates T-cell differentiation and restores balance between Th1/Th2 pathways. It has demonstrated immune-rebalancing effects in chronic inflammatory and autoimmune conditions without broad immunosuppression. FDA-approved in over 30 countries for hepatitis B/C; used off-label in integrative dermatology protocols.

Gordon, K. B., Strober, B., Lebwohl, M., et al. (2018). Efficacy and safety of risankizumab in moderate-to-severe plaque psoriasis (UltIMMa-1 and UltIMMa-2): results from two double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled and ustekinumab-controlled phase 3 trials. The Lancet, 392(10148), 650–661. Risankizumab (Skyrizi) achieved PASI 90 response in approximately 75% of patients at 16 weeks, significantly outperforming both placebo and ustekinumab. As a selective IL-23 inhibitor, it targets a single upstream cytokine rather than broadly suppressing immune function.

Harari, M., et al. (2000). Climatotherapy of atopic dermatitis at the Dead Sea: demographic evaluation and cost-effectiveness. International Journal of Dermatology, 39(1), 59–69; Shani, J., et al. (1997). Dead Sea climatotherapy for psoriasis. Journal of the American Academy of Dermatology, 37(6), 828–835. Multiple studies over three decades report remission rates of 70–80% in psoriasis patients after 4-week Dead Sea treatment courses combining UV exposure, mineral-rich water, and reduced atmospheric allergen load. Benefits typically persist 3–6 months post-treatment.
